It's nice to meet you; what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Nice to meet you, Dr. Smith. Well, I came in because ever since they changed my hours at the warehouse and put in this new inventory system, I just feel like I’m falling apart. \n\nI'm missing shifts, I've been skipping out on my church commitments, and I keep snapping at my family. Some mornings... I just sit there in my work boots, wondering if I can even face going into the place anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before, or is this the first time you've experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: No, this is really the first time. I mean, I've had stressful things happen before, sure, but nothing like this. I've always been able to just put my head down and get through it. But right now... I just can't seem to shake this feeling. It's all new to me, and honestly, it's pretty overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: When you are sitting there in your work boots feeling overwhelmed, do you find that your heart is racing or that you feel physically restless and on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say my heart races or anything like that. I don't feel jittery or restless, either. It's more like... I just feel heavy. Like I'm stuck right there in the chair and I just can't get myself to move. I don't really have a lot of racing thoughts or excessive worry when I'm away from it, I just... I just hit a wall when it's time to actually go in. So, no, I don't think I'm worrying about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Even if you aren't feeling constant worry, do you find that you have been feeling more nervous or tense than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt nervous or tense, either. Like I said, it's not a keyed-up kind of feeling. It's just that heavy, drained feeling. I'm not walking around tense or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where you feel especially overwhelmed by a sense of dread or apprehension, even if it's not the same as being 'tense' or 'nervous'?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I really wouldn't call it dread or apprehension. Like I was saying, it's just... I just feel completely out of gas. It's nice to meet you; what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Nice to meet you, Dr. Smith. Well, I came in because ever since they changed my hours at the warehouse and put in this new inventory system, I just feel like I’m falling apart. \n\nI'm missing shifts, I've been skipping out on my church commitments, and I keep snapping at my family. Some mornings... I just sit there in my work boots, wondering if I can even face going into the place anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before, or is this the first time you've experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: No, this is really the first time. I mean, I've had stressful things happen before, sure, but nothing like this. I've always been able to just put my head down and get through it. But right now... I just can't seem to shake this feeling. It's all new to me, and honestly, it's pretty overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: When you are sitting there in your work boots feeling overwhelmed, do you find that your heart is racing or that you feel physically restless and on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say my heart races or anything like that. I don't feel jittery or restless, either. It's more like... I just feel heavy. Like I'm stuck right there in the chair and I just can't get myself to move. I don't feel on edge, just... drained, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively or feeling fearful about the changes at work, even when you aren't sitting there feeling drained?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I'm fearful or... or worrying about it all the time. I know how to do the work. It's not like I'm sitting at home scared of the new system or the hours. \n\nIt's just that heavy feeling I mentioned before. I don't really have a lot of racing thoughts or excessive worry when I'm away from it, I just... I just hit a wall when it's time to actually go in. So, no, I don't think I'm worrying about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Even if you aren't feeling constant worry, do you find that you have been feeling more nervous or tense than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt nervous or tense, either. Like I said, it's not a keyed-up kind of feeling. It's just that heavy, drained feeling. I'm not walking around tense or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where you feel especially overwhelmed by a sense of dread or apprehension, even if it's not the same as being 'tense' or 'nervous'?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I really wouldn't call it dread or apprehension. Like I was saying, it's just... I just feel completely out of gas. There's no fear or dread about the job itself, just that total lack of energy to actually get up and go do it.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these changes at work, do you find yourself feeling sad, tearful, or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad or tearful. And not hopeless, really. It's just... like I said, just that heavy feeling. I'm not sitting around crying about it or feeling down in the dumps. I'm just completely drained when I think about going in.\n\nDoctor: When you say you have been snapping at your family and missing your church commitments, would you say that your behavior toward others has changed significantly compared to how you were before these work changes started?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd say that. I don't think my behavior toward other people has changed all that much, and I haven't really been snapping at my family. It's really just what I was saying before—I just don't have the energy for much of anything right now. So maybe I'm just keeping to myself a bit more because I'm so drained, but I wouldn't say I'm treating people any differently.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the lack of energy, would you say that you have been acting in a way that is out of character for you, such as being rebellious, impulsive, or failing to follow rules or expectations at work or at home?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't been acting out or breaking rules, or doing anything impulsive. I'm just a 74-year-old man who's too tired to keep up with the changes at work. I go in, do what I can, and come home. There's no rebellion or anything out of character... just the exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that your work performance or your ability to keep up with your daily responsibilities has been significantly impaired because of this overwhelming sense of exhaustion?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. At work, my supervisor actually... well, he's documented some problems with my reliability lately. It's gotten to the point where he even talked to me about taking medical leave. And outside of work, I've had to pull back from my church duties. People there really counted on me for those things, but I just can't manage them right now with how drained I am.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling this exhaustion and struggling with your responsibilities, do you find yourself feeling more irritable or frustrated than you typically would?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel angry or... irritable. Like I said before, I haven't been snapping at my family or anything like that. It's really just the exhaustion. I'm honestly just too tired to even get worked up or frustrated about things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

People there really counted on me for those things, but I just can't manage them right now with how drained I am.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling this exhaustion and struggling with your responsibilities, do you find yourself feeling more irritable or frustrated than you typically would?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Well, I came in because ever since they changed my hours at the warehouse and put in this new inventory system, I just feel like I’m falling apart. \n\nI'm missing shifts, I've been skipping out on my church commitments, and I keep snapping at my family. Some mornings... I just sit there in my work boots, wondering if I can even face going into the place anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before, or is this the first time you've experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: No, this is really the first time. I mean, I've had stressful things happen before, sure, but nothing like this. I've always been able to just put my head down and get through it. But right now... I just can't seem to shake this feeling. It's all new to me, and honestly, it's pretty overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: When you are sitting there in your work boots feeling overwhelmed, do you find that your heart is racing or that you feel physically restless and on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say my heart races or anything like that. I don't feel jittery or restless, either. It's more like... I just feel heavy. Like I'm stuck right there in the chair and I just can't get myself to move. I don't feel on edge, just... drained, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively or feeling fearful about the changes at work, even when you aren't sitting there feeling drained?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I'm fearful or... or worrying about it all the time. I know how to do the work. I don't really have a lot of racing thoughts or excessive worry when I'm away from it, I just... I just hit a wall when it's time to actually go in. So, no, I don't think I'm worrying about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Even if you aren't feeling constant worry, do you find that you have been feeling more nervous or tense than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt nervous or tense, either. Like I said, it's not a keyed-up kind of feeling. It's just that heavy, drained feeling. I'm not walking around tense or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where you feel especially overwhelmed by a sense of dread or apprehension, even if it's not the same as being 'tense' or 'nervous'?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I haven't really felt nervous or tense, either. Like I said, it's not a keyed-up kind of feeling. It's just that heavy, drained feeling. I'm not walking around tense or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where you feel especially overwhelmed by a sense of dread or apprehension, even if it's not the same as being 'tense' or 'nervous'?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. It's all new to me, and honestly, it's pretty overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: When you are sitting there in your work boots feeling overwhelmed, do you find that your heart is racing or that you feel physically restless and on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say my heart races or anything like that. I don't feel jittery or restless, either. It's more like... I just feel heavy. Like I'm stuck right there in the chair and I just can't get myself to move. I don't feel on edge, just... drained, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively or feeling fearful about the changes at work, even when you aren't sitting there feeling drained?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I'm fearful or... or worrying about it all the time. I know how to do the work. It's not like I'm sitting at home scared of the new system or the hours. \n\nIt's just that heavy feeling I mentioned before. I don't really have a lot of racing thoughts or excessive worry when I'm away from it, I just... I just hit a wall when it's time to actually go in. So, no, I don't think I'm worrying about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Even if you aren't feeling constant worry, do you find that you have been feeling more nervous or tense than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt nervous or tense, either. Like I said, it's not a keyed-up kind of feeling. It's just that heavy, drained feeling. I'm not walking around tense or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where you feel especially overwhelmed by a sense of dread or apprehension, even if it's not the same as being 'tense' or 'nervous'?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I really wouldn't call it dread or apprehension. Like I was saying, it's just... I just feel completely out of gas. There's no fear or dread about the job itself, just that total lack of energy to actually get up and go do it.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these changes at work, do you find yourself feeling sad, tearful, or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad or tearful. And not hopeless, really. It's just... like I said, just that heavy feeling. I'm not sitting around crying about it or feeling down in the dumps. I'm just completely drained when I think about going in.\n\nDoctor: When you say you have been snapping at your family and missing your church commitments, would you say that your behavior toward others has changed significantly compared to how you were before these work changes started?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd say that. I don't think my behavior toward other people has changed all that much, and I haven't really been snapping at my family. It's really just what I was saying before—I just don't have the energy for much of anything right now. So maybe I'm just keeping to myself a bit more because I'm so drained, but I wouldn't say I'm treating people any differently.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the lack of energy, would you say that you have been acting in a way that is out of character for you, such as being rebellious, impulsive, or failing to follow rules or expectations at work or at home?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't been acting out or breaking rules, or doing anything impulsive. I'm just a 74-year-old man who's too tired to keep up with the changes at work. I go in, do what I can, and come home. There's no rebellion or anything out of character... just the exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that your work performance or your ability to keep up with your daily responsibilities has been significantly impaired because of this overwhelming sense of exhaustion?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. At work, my supervisor actually... well, he's documented some problems with my reliability lately. It's gotten to the point where he even talked to me about taking medical leave. And outside of work, I've had to pull back from my church duties. People there really counted on me for those things, but I just can't manage them right now with how drained I am.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling this exhaustion and struggling with your responsibilities, do you find yourself feeling more irritable or frustrated than you typically would?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel angry or... irritable. Like I said before, I haven't been snapping at my family or anything like that. It's really just the exhaustion. I'm honestly just too tired to even get worked up or frustrated about things.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling this exhaustion, do you ever feel a sense of low mood or general unhappiness?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel unhappy or down, exactly. It's not a sadness. I just don't have the energy for anything. I'm just drained, not depressed.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the exhaustion and withdrawal from your activities, have you found yourself struggling to follow through with the specific expectations or duties required of you at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I mentioned a few minutes ago, that's exactly what's happening. At work, my supervisor has actually been documenting those problems with my reliability. I'm just not keeping up with the demands of the job, which is why he sat me down and brought up taking medical leave. And it's the same thing with the church duties I told you about. People were really counting on me to handle those specific responsibilities, and I've just had to pull back completely because I can't follow through with any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself acting in a way that is contrary to social norms, such as being disruptive, reckless, or violating the rights of others, even if you don't feel angry?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't been doing anything reckless or disruptive. Like I said, I'm really just too tired to do much of anything, let alone cause trouble or act out. I just go to work, struggle through it, and come home to rest. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that you are intentionally or unintentionally failing to show up for your responsibilities, such as missing shifts or skipping church, specifically because you just cannot bring yourself to go, even though you know you are expected to be there?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. Ever since they changed my hours at the warehouse and put in that new inventory system, I just feel like I’m falling apart. So, yes, I am missing shifts and my church commitments. \n\nSome mornings I get dressed, and I just sit there in my work boots... just wondering if I can even face the place anymore. I just can't seem to handle it all right now.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you've been missing shifts and church commitments; besides these absences, have you been acting in a way that goes against the expectations or rules set for you, such as being rebellious or disregarding the rights of others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been rebellious or anything like that. I'm not breaking rules or bothering anybody else. I'm just... I'm just not showing up because I can't manage it, not because I'm trying to be defiant. I just stay home.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the exhaustion, have you been experiencing any unexplained physical aches or pains lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any of that. I don't really have any aches or pains that I can't explain. It's just the tiredness, really. Physically, I feel fine, just... drained.\n\nDoctor: When you are sitting there feeling this overwhelming exhaustion, do you find that your appetite has changed, or that your sleep patterns have been disrupted?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite is about the same as always. I'm still eating my regular meals. And my sleep hasn't really changed either. I sleep through the night fine. I'm just so tired during the day, but it's not because I'm missing meals or losing sleep.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the fact that you are missing your work shifts and church commitments, do you feel a sense of distress that is out of proportion to the severity of the work changes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's all new to me, and honestly, it's pretty overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: When you are sitting there in your work boots feeling overwhelmed, do you find that your heart is racing or that you feel physically restless and on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say my heart races or anything like that. I don't feel jittery or restless, either. It's more like... I just feel heavy. Like I'm stuck right there in the chair and I just can't get myself to move. I don't feel on edge, just... drained, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively or feeling fearful about the changes at work, even when you aren't sitting there feeling drained?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I'm fearful or... or worrying about it all the time. I know how to do the work. It's not like I'm sitting at home scared of the new system or the hours. \n\nIt's just that heavy feeling I mentioned before. I don't really have a lot of racing thoughts or excessive worry when I'm away from it, I just... I just hit a wall when it's time to actually go in. So, no, I don't think I'm worrying about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Even if you aren't feeling constant worry, do you find that you have been feeling more nervous or tense than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really felt nervous or tense, either. Like I said, it's not a keyed-up kind of feeling. It's just that heavy, drained feeling. I'm not walking around tense or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where you feel especially overwhelmed by a sense of dread or apprehension, even if it's not the same as being 'tense' or 'nervous'?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I really wouldn't call it dread or apprehension. Like I was saying, it's just... I just feel completely out of gas. There's no fear or dread about the job itself, just that total lack of energy to actually get up and go do it.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these changes at work, do you find yourself feeling sad, tearful, or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad or tearful. And not hopeless, really. It's just... like I said, just that heavy feeling. I'm not sitting around crying about it or feeling down in the dumps. I'm just completely drained when I think about going in.\n\nDoctor: When you say you have been snapping at your family and missing your church commitments, would you say that your behavior toward others has changed significantly compared to how you were before these work changes started?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd say that. I don't think my behavior toward other people has changed all that much, and I haven't really been snapping at my family. It's really just what I was saying before—I just don't have the energy for much of anything right now. So maybe I'm just keeping to myself a bit more because I'm so drained, but I wouldn't say I'm treating people any differently.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the lack of energy, would you say that you have been acting in a way that is out of character for you, such as being rebellious, impulsive, or failing to follow rules or expectations at work or at home?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't been acting out or breaking rules, or doing anything impulsive. I'm just a 74-year-old man who's too tired to keep up with the changes at work. I go in, do what I can, and come home. There's no rebellion or anything out of character... just the exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that your work performance or your ability to keep up with your daily responsibilities has been significantly impaired because of this overwhelming sense of exhaustion?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. At work, my supervisor actually... well, he's documented some problems with my reliability lately. It's gotten to the point where he even talked to me about taking medical leave. And outside of work, I've had to pull back from my church duties. People there really counted on me for those things, but I just can't manage them right now with how drained I am.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling this exhaustion and struggling with your responsibilities, do you find yourself feeling more irritable or frustrated than you typically would?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel angry or... irritable. Like I said before, I haven't been snapping at my family or anything like that. It's really just the exhaustion. I'm honestly just too tired to even get worked up or frustrated about things.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling this exhaustion, do you ever feel a sense of low mood or general unhappiness?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel unhappy or down, exactly. It's not a sadness. I just don't have the energy for anything. I'm just drained, not depressed.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the exhaustion and withdrawal from your activities, have you found yourself struggling to follow through with the specific expectations or duties required of you at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I mentioned a few minutes ago, that's exactly what's happening. At work, my supervisor has actually been documenting those problems with my reliability. I'm just not keeping up with the demands of the job, which is why he sat me down and brought up taking medical leave. And it's the same thing with the church duties I told you about. People were really counting on me to handle those specific responsibilities, and I've just had to pull back completely because I can't follow through with any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself acting in a way that is contrary to social norms, such as being disruptive, reckless, or violating the rights of others, even if you don't feel angry?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't been doing anything reckless or disruptive. Like I said, I'm really just too tired to do much of anything, let alone cause trouble or act out. I just go to work, struggle through it, and come home to rest. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding that you are intentionally or unintentionally failing to show up for your responsibilities, such as missing shifts or skipping church, specifically because you just cannot bring yourself to go, even though you know you are expected to be there?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. Ever since they changed my hours at the warehouse and put in that new inventory system, I just feel like I’m falling apart. So, yes, I am missing shifts and my church commitments. \n\nSome mornings I get dressed, and I just sit there in my work boots... just wondering if I can even face the place anymore. I know I'm expected to be there, but I just can't bring myself to go out the door. It's getting to where I'm even snapping at my family. I just can't seem to handle it all right now.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you've been missing shifts and church commitments; besides these absences, have you been acting in a way that goes against the expectations or rules set for you, such as being rebellious or disregarding the rights of others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been rebellious or anything like that. I'm not breaking rules or bothering anybody else. I'm just... I'm just not showing up because I can't manage it, not because I'm trying to be defiant. I just stay home.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the exhaustion, have you been experiencing any unexplained physical aches or pains lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any of that. I don't really have any aches or pains that I can't explain. It's just the tiredness, really. Physically, I feel fine, just... drained.\n\nDoctor: When you are sitting there feeling this overwhelming exhaustion, do you find that your appetite has changed, or that your sleep patterns have been disrupted?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite is about the same as always. I'm still eating my regular meals. And my sleep hasn't really changed either. I sleep through the night fine. I'm just so tired during the day, but it's not because I'm missing meals or losing sleep.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the fact that you are missing your work shifts and church commitments, do you feel a sense of distress that is out of proportion to the severity of the work changes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel bad about missing my shifts and not being at church, sure. But I wouldn't say the distress is out of proportion. It just feels like... a normal amount of worry for a guy who isn't showing up to do his job. I don't think I'm overreacting to it, if that's what you mean. I just know I'm falling behind and that bothers me, but it makes sense that it would.\n\nDoctor: When you miss these shifts or church commitments, do the people involved express that they are upset or angry with you, or is your withdrawal generally accepted by them?\n\nPatient: Well, they aren't just accepting it. My supervisor at work actually sat me down recently. He's documented the issues with my reliability because I've been missing so many shifts. He even brought up the idea of me taking medical leave because it's becoming a real problem for them. And at church... I've pulled back from duties that people were really counting on me to handle.
